### Lessons (printed page ranges from the book's own Contents, verified against lesson starts)

| Lesson | Title | Printed pp. |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Function | 1–13 |
| 2 | Polynomial | 14–25 |
| 3 | Linear Programming | 26–35 |
| 4 | Quadratic Equation | 36–50 |
| 5 | Surd | 51–55 |
| 6 | Matrix and Determinant | 56–84 |
| 7 | Trigonometry | 85–138 |
| 8 | Coordinate Geometry | 139–174 |
| 9 | Transformation | 175–216 |
| 10 | Vector | 217–241 |
| 11 | Statistics | 242–274 |
| 12 | Continuity | 275–293 |

### What each lesson teaches (section by section)

- **L1 Function (pp. 1–13).** 1.1 introduction (function as a special
  relation; Leibniz 1673, Euler y = f(x) 1730); 1.2 composite function
  (g∘f, mapping diagrams); 1.3 inverse function. Exercises 1.1 (p. 5),
  1.2 (p. 11). Builds directly on Grade 9 Optional Lesson 1.
- **L2 Polynomial (pp. 14–25).** 2.1 introduction; 2.2 remainder theorem;
  2.3 factor theorem; 2.4 rational root theorem. Exercises 2.1 (p. 18),
  2.2 (p. 22).
- **L3 Linear Programming (pp. 26–35).** 3.1 introduction; 3.2 linear
  inequalities; 3.3 system of linear inequalities; 3.4 solution of
  linear programming problems (graphical method, feasible region,
  objective function, corner-point method — two variables only, no
  simplex method). Exercise 3 (p. 33).
- **L4 Quadratic Equation (pp. 36–50).** 4.1 introduction; 4.2 converting
  y = ax²+bx+c into y = a(x−h)²+k; 4.3 sketching the graph of
  y = a(x−h)²+k; 4.4 transformation of y = x²; Exercise 4.1 (p. 44);
  4.5 solution of a quadratic equation using graphs; 4.6 solution of a
  quadratic and a linear equation using graphs. Vertex form and
  graphical solutions — no quadratic formula derivation here (the
  formula is used, not proved).
- **L5 Surd (pp. 51–55).** 5.1 introduction; 5.2 equations involving
  surds. Short lesson (5 pages) — solving equations with square roots.
- **L6 Matrix and Determinant (pp. 56–84).** 6.1 introduction; 6.2
  transpose of matrix (6.1.1 properties of transpose — note the
  sub-numbering slip: this should be 6.2.1); 6.2 multiplication of
  matrix; 6.3 determinant of a matrix (6.3.1 order one, 6.3.2 order two,
  6.3.3 singular matrix, 6.3.4 non-singular matrix); 6.4 inverse matrix
  (6.4.1 process of finding the inverse of a 2×2 matrix — swap the
  diagonal, change signs, divide by the determinant; 6.4.2 properties
  of inverse matrix, including (AB)⁻¹ = B⁻¹A⁻¹ and (A⁻¹)⁻¹ = A).
  Worked examples verified: Example 1 (printed p. 78) uses A = [[2,3],[1,4]], |A| = 4×2−3×1 = 5,
  A⁻¹ = (1/5)[[4,−3],[−1,2]] (correct). (A = [[4,3],[1,2]] is the matrix of Activity 1, where the
  properties are to be verified, not worked out; its inverse (1/5)[[2,−3],[−1,4]] is not computed
  in the book.) Inverse-from-given-matrix
  example solving 18m−35 = 1 → m = 2 and 5n+36 = 1 → n = −7 (correct).
  Only 2×2 matrices — no 3×3 determinants or inverses.
- **L7 Trigonometry (pp. 85–138).** 7.1 introduction; 7.2 compound angles
  (7.2.1 ratios of A+B and A−B); 7.3 multiple angles; 7.4 submultiple
  angles; 7.5 transformation of trigonometric identities (sum-to-product
  / product-to-sum); 7.6 conditional identities; 7.7 height and distance
  (7.7.1 angles of elevation and depression). The longest lesson in the
  book (54 pages). No inverse trig functions.
- **L8 Coordinate Geometry (pp. 139–174).** 8.0 introduction; 8.1 equation
  of lines through two points; 8.2 angle between two straight lines;
  8.3 conic section (8.3.1 parts of a cone — introduction only, no
  parabola/ellipse/hyperbola equations worked out as separate topics);
  8.4 circle (8.4.1 equation of a circle). Straight lines and circles
  are the working content; conics are named and described, not
  developed.
- **L9 Transformation (pp. 175–216).** 9.1 introduction; 9.2 translation
  of geometrical shapes; 9.3 combined transformation (9.3.1 two identical
  transformations, 9.3.2 two different transformations); 9.4
  transformation using matrix (9.4.1 translation using a 2×1 matrix,
  9.4.2 transformation using a 2×2 matrix); 9.3.3 introduction to unit
  square (see slip below). The longest transformation treatment in the
  set — reflection, rotation, enlargement and translation, combined and
  in matrix form. 42 pages.
- **L10 Vector (pp. 217–241).** 10.1 introduction; 10.2 scalar product
  (10.2.1 introduction, 10.2.2 parallel/perpendicular conditions,
  10.2.3 scalar product in coordinates, 10.2.4 geometrical meaning,
  10.2.5 properties, 10.2.6 triangle law of vector addition); 10.3.1
  mid-point formula; 10.3.2 internal division theorem; 10.3.3 external
  division theorem; 10.4 theorems (vector proofs, e.g. mid-point and
  division theorems proved by vectors). Dot product is the new content
  vs Grade 9 Optional; no cross (vector) product.
- **L11 Statistics (pp. 242–274).** 11.1 introduction (history: Graunt,
  Fisher as "Father of Statistics", Pearson, Gosset); 11.2 quartile
  deviation; 11.3 quartile deviation of continuous series; 11.4 mean
  deviation (11.4.1 uses of standard deviation, 11.4.2 coefficient of
  standard deviation); 11.5 coefficient of variation; 11.6 whisker box
  plot (11.2.2 comparison of two data using whisker box plot — note the
  sub-numbering slip). Continuous series is the new content vs Grade 9
  Optional. Worked answers include factory comparison (Factory B spends
  more; Factory B more uniform) and a 36-student exam-score set
  (mean 45.28, SD 19.07, CV 42.1%).
- **L12 Continuity (pp. 275–293).** 12.1 introduction (informal "draw
  without lifting the pen" definition; Aristotle/Newton/Euler/Cauchy/
  Weierstrass named); 12.2 continuity and discontinuity (left-hand and
  right-hand limits, the three conditions for continuity at a point:
  lim₋ = lim₊ = f(a)). Worked example verified: f(x) = (x²+x−6)/(x+3),
  lim x→−3 f(x) = −5 from both sides (table of values −3.1 → −5.1 …
  −2.9999 → −4.9999), but f(−3) is 0/0, undefined — so f is not
  continuous at x = −3 (correct). Exercises 12.1 (p. 277), 12.2 (p. 286);
  project work (temperature graph, p. 289); miscellaneous exercise
  (p. 289). Calculus stops here: limits and continuity only — **no
  differentiation, no integration, no derivatives of any kind.**

### Answers

- Answers are printed at the end of each lesson (e.g. Lesson 11 answers
  on printed p. 265, Lesson 12 answers on p. 293).

### Slips in this book

- **"Exercise 10 .1"** (printed p. 225) — spacing typo in the label.
- **Out-of-order numbering:** "9.3.3 Introduction to Unit Square"
  (p. 205) appears AFTER §9.4 (p. 201). It belongs under §9.3.
- **Duplicate exercise label:** "Exercise 11.2" is used twice — once at
  printed p. 253 (after §11.4 Mean Deviation) and again at p. 270
  (after §11.6 Whisker Box Plot). The second should have been
  Exercise 11.4 or 11.5.
- **Sub-numbering slips:** "6.1.1 Properties of Transpose of Matrix"
  (p. 57) sits under §6.2, not §6.1; "11.2.2 Comparison of Two Data
  using Whisker Box Plot" (p. 268) sits under §11.6, not §11.2.
- **Exercise 12.2 Q1D, option (c)** (p. 287): the correct option is
  printed as "lim x→c⁻ f(x) = lim x→c⁺ f(x) = f(a)" — it mixes c and a
  in one statement; it should read f(c). The intended answer is still
  clear, but the option as printed is sloppy.
- **Soft history, Lesson 12:** "Aristotle first discussed the idea of
  continuity" and "in the seventeenth century, Isaac Newton proved that
  motion is continuous" are loose popular-history claims, not precise
  history. Not maths errors; do not repeat as fact.
- No wrong worked maths answer was found in the examples re-checked.

### Honest negatives (Optional Grade 10)

- No logarithms. No complex numbers. No 3×3 matrices/determinants.
  No inverse trigonometric functions. No differentiation, no
  integration, no differential equations — continuity is the stopping
  point. No probability at all (no probability unit in Optional Grade
  10 either; probability lives only in the compulsory books). No
  permutations/combinations. Conic sections are introduced, not
  developed (no parabola/ellipse/hyperbola equations).

## Book 5 — g10-teacher-guide.pdf — Grade 10 Teacher Guide, Compulsory Mathematics, Nepali medium

### Identity as printed

- Cover (PDF p. 1, seen visually): शिक्षक निर्देशिका / अनिवार्य गणित /
  कक्षा १० — "Teacher Guide / Compulsory Mathematics / Class 10".
  Writing group ("लेखन समूह"): **कृष्ण गोसाईं (Krishna Gosain), तेज
  प्रसाद ढकाल (Tej Prasad Dhakal), वासुदेव दवाडी (Basudev Dawadi),
  सुमित्रा भट्ट (Sumitra Bhatt)**. Publisher: नेपाल सरकार / पाठ्यक्रम
  विकास केन्द्र / सानोठिमी, भक्तपुर — Government of Nepal, Curriculum
  Development Centre, Sanothimi, Bhaktapur. Year printed on cover:
  **२०७४ (2074 BS)**. A large pink **DRAFT** watermark runs diagonally
  across every page.
- Edition: the cover year 2074 is the only date; there is no "First
  Edition" line and no ISBN anywhere in the file (searched whole file).
  Record as: 2074, DRAFT, ISBN none printed.
- PDF pages: 237. Printed pages: 1–236, continuous (verified folio by
  folio).
- Printed-page offset: **+1** (PDF p. 2 = printed p. 1; verified again at
  PDF p. 237 = printed p. 236).
- Language: Nepali (Devanagari) throughout.
- No contents page: the book starts directly with Unit 1 on printed p. 1.
- Completeness: **complete as a draft, except 7 blank pages** (see
  defects below). All 18 units are present and the book ends with a model
  question paper (printed p. 236).

### Structure

- Every unit follows the same template: introduction (परिचय), competency
  (सक्षमता), teaching-learning activities (शिक्षण सिकाइ क्रियाकलाप, with
  worked classroom activities, discussion questions and board work),
  content division by period (विषयवस्तुको विभाजन), evaluation
  (मूल्याङ्कन), homework / project work (गृहकार्य / परियोजना कार्य), and
  a unit test (एकाइ परीक्षा, with a question paper and marks).
- Estimated periods per unit (as printed in each unit header):

| Unit | Title | Periods | Guide PDF pp. (printed pp.) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sets | 8 | 2–15 (1–14) |
| 2 | Tax and Money Exchange | 7 | 16–25 (15–24) |
| 3 | Compound Interest | 6 | 26–34 (25–33) |
| 4 | Population Growth and Compound Depreciation | 7 | 35–44 (34–43) |
| 5 | Plane Surface | 7 | 45–57 (44–56) |
| 6 | Cylinder and Sphere | 10 | 58–73 (57–72) |
| 7 | Prism and Pyramid | 11 | 74–94 (73–93) |
| 8 | HCF and LCM | 6 | 95–103 (94–102) |
| 9 | Radical and Surd | 5 | 104–113 (103–112) |
| 10 | Indices | 6 | 114–122 (113–121) |
| 11 | Algebraic Fraction | 7 | 123–129 (122–128) |
| 12 | Equation | 9 | 130–140 (129–139) |
| 13 | Triangle and Quadrilateral | 12 | 141–152 (140–151) |
| 14 | Construction | 12 | 153–160 (152–159) |
| 15 | Circle | 22 | 161–179 (160–178) |
| 16 | Trigonometry | 10 | 180–194 (179–193) |
| 17 | Statistics | 16 | 195–221 (194–220) |
| 18 | Probability | 11 | 222–237 (221–236) |
| **Total** | | **172 periods** | |

- Unit 15 (Circle, 22 periods) is the longest allocation; Unit 9 (5
  periods) the shortest. Unit 15's introduction notes that circle
  properties were treated in Euclid's Elements, Book 3 (300 BCE).
- Unit 3 states its scope explicitly: compound interest "annual up to 3
  years, half-yearly up to 2 years" — matching the student book's
  Exercise 3.
- The guide ends (printed p. 236) with a model question paper
  ("नमुना प्रश्नपत्र") for the unit test, after Unit 18's Period 11.

### TEACHER-GUIDE COMPANION VERDICT: TRUE COMPANION (with two caveats)

- **Verdict: this guide IS keyed to the held Grade 10 student book.**
  Evidence: (1) same 18 units, in the same order, with the same titles —
  including Unit 9 "Radical and Surd" and Unit 15 Circle; (2) the guide
  cites the student book's exercises by number and tells the teacher to
  get them solved — e.g. Unit 1, Period 3: "textbook Exercise 1.1,
  questions 1 and 2 — get them solved and brought"; Unit 18, Period 9:
  "get textbook Exercise 18.2 question 8 done"; (3) the guide's worked examples do NOT reuse the student book's numbers or contexts — its Unit 1
  survey is 300 students (120 table tennis, 205 basketball), not the student book's 52-student
  cricket/football survey, and neither the VAT invoice numbers (Rs. 27,876.10 / Rs. 31,500) nor the
  exchange-rate numbers (1947.55 AUD × 80.871) appear anywhere in the guide; (4) one author overlaps — Krishna Gosain is both a student-book
  author and the guide's first-named writer.
- This is the OPPOSITE of the Nepal English pattern, where the guides
  mostly keyed to newer/different books (only Grade 3's guide was a true
  companion, and the English G10 guide was a "Feedback Copy" keyed to a
  newer edition). The maths G10 guide matches its student book.
- **Caveat 1 — it is a DRAFT, dated 2074.** The held student book
  carries no edition year at all, so edition-matching cannot be proven
  from the student-book side; the guide predates the Grade 9 first
  edition (2079) and the Optional books (2082/2083). What can be said is
  that the guide's CONTENT matches the held student book unit for unit —
  it is not keyed to a different book.
- **Caveat 2 — 7 blank pages.** Printed pp. 17 and 19 (Unit 2, inside
  the money-exchange material) and printed pp. 181–185 (Unit 16,
  inside the trigonometry content-division section) are blank: verified
  visually — nothing but the DRAFT watermark — and confirmed in the file
  (0 images, 0 drawings, text length 5, i.e. only the word "DRAFT").
  Content that should sit on those pages is missing from the held copy.
  The guide is usable, but its Unit 2 and Unit 16 sections are
  physically incomplete.

### Held-copy defects (all five books)

- **Teacher guide:** 7 genuinely blank pages — printed pp. 17, 19,
  181, 182, 183, 184, 185 (PDF pp. 18, 20, 182–186). DRAFT watermark
  only. No other defects in the guide.
- **G9 student book:** the ISBN is unfinished/blank (see Book 1); no
  other defect. Cover is plain white with no illustration (cosmetic).
- **G10 student book:** no imprint/edition page at all (see Book 2);
  no other defect. No missing, clipped or truncated pages.
- **Optional books:** no defects. Page sequences verified continuous
  (G9-opt 1–290; G10-opt 1–293).
- **No Grade 9 teacher guide exists in this set.** Confirmed: the
  set-verification lists teacher guides only for Grades 2, 5, 8 and 10;
  no G9 guide file is held, and there is no G9 guide inside the Band C
  files. (The English phase owed a complete Grade 9 teacher's guide;
  the maths set does not supply one either.)

## The compulsory-vs-optional split on advanced topics

Checked by whole-file keyword search plus the unit-by-unit read. "No"
below means the topic does not appear anywhere in that book (not even
as a mention), unless noted.

| Topic | Compulsory G9 | Compulsory G10 | Optional G9 | Optional G10 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Functions (as mappings) | NO | NO | YES — relations, functions, domain/range, inverse relation, graphs y=xⁿ | YES — composite, inverse functions |
| Logarithms | NO | NO | NO | NO |
| Matrices | NO | NO | YES — intro, add/subtract, scalar mult. only | YES — transpose, multiplication, determinant (1×1, 2×2), inverse 2×2 |
| Vectors | NO | NO | YES — intro, triangle law, scalar mult., magnitude/direction/unit vector | YES — incl. scalar (dot) product, division theorems |
| Complex numbers | NO | NO | NO | NO |
| Trigonometry beyond right triangles | NO — ratios + special angles only | NO — + area by trig, height/distance only | YES — all quadrants, identities, radian/centesimal measure | YES — compound/multiple/submultiple angles, conditional identities |
| Probability distributions | NO | NO | NO (no probability unit) | NO (no probability unit) |
| Calculus | NO | NO | YES — limits only (infinity, limit of algebraic function) | YES — limits + continuity (no derivatives) |
| Differential equations | NO | NO | NO | NO |
| Coordinate geometry | NO | NO | YES — straight lines, locus, transformations | YES — lines, angle between lines, circle equations, conics introduced |
| Linear programming | NO | NO | NO | YES — graphical, 2 variables |
| Statistics beyond mean/median/mode | NO — mean/median/mode/quartiles, histogram, ogive | NO — grouped mean, median, quartiles | YES — quartile/mean/SD, CV, box plots (discrete) | YES — incl. continuous series, box plots |

- **Net: the compulsory G9+G10 set teaches NO functions, NO logarithms,
  NO matrices, NO vectors, NO complex numbers, NO calculus and NO
  differential equations at all.** The advanced topics live entirely in
  the Optional books — and even there, logarithms, complex numbers,
  differential equations, differentiation/integration and probability
  distributions are never taught. The most advanced content in the whole
  band is Optional G10 Lesson 12 (continuity) and Lesson 6 (2×2 matrix
  inverses) — calculus stops at continuity, with no derivative anywhere.

## Book errors found (whole band)

1. **G10 compulsory, Unit 1, Example 2, printed pp. 2–3 — CONFIRMED,
   serious.** Survey data inconsistent: 52 students, 28 cricket, 24
   football, 18 both, 6 neither. Regions sum to 40, not 52. Correct
   "neither" = **18** (or total = 40). The book's Venn diagram repeats
   the wrong 6. Never teach as fact.
2. **G10 Optional, Exercise 12.2 Q1D option (c), printed p. 287 — minor.**
   Correct option printed as "lim x→c⁻ f(x) = lim x→c⁺ f(x) = f(a)";
   should be f(c), not f(a).
3. **G9 compulsory, Exercise numbering, Unit 20 — minor.** Exercises
   numbered 20.1 then 20.3; no Exercise 20.2 exists.
4. **G9 Optional numbering slips — minor.** §2.2.1, §3.1.1, §3.2.1 missing;
   "Exercise 1.1 (H)" used twice (pp. 33, 36); box-plot sections numbered
   5.3/5.4 duplicating §5.4 (Standard Deviation).
5. **G10 Optional numbering slips — minor.** "Exercise 10 .1" spacing
   typo (p. 225); "9.3.3 Introduction to Unit Square" placed after §9.4
   (p. 205); "Exercise 11.2" used twice (pp. 253, 270); "6.1.1
   Properties of Transpose" under §6.2; "11.2.2 Comparison of Two Data"
   under §11.6.
6. **G10 Optional, Lesson 12 history — soft, not maths.** "Aristotle
   first discussed continuity" / "Newton proved motion is continuous"
   are loose popular claims; do not repeat as fact.
7. **G10 Optional, Lesson 6 sub-numbering — minor.** "6.1.1 Properties
   of Transpose of Matrix" sits under §6.2.

No other wrong worked answer was found in any book. In particular the
following were re-checked and are correct: G9 VAT invoice and VAT chain
(13% at each stage, total 2,340); G10 money-exchange example
(1947.55 × 80.871 = 157,500.32); G10 answer-key Exercise 1.1 Q1 (all ten
parts agree with its Venn diagram); G9 Optional angle conversions
(60°15'30" = 216,930"); G10 Optional 2×2 inverse (A=[[4,3],[1,2]],
|A|=5, A⁻¹=(1/5)[[2,−3],[−1,4]]; m=2, n=−7 in the inverse-matrix
example); G10 Optional continuity example (limit −5 at x=−3, function
undefined there, hence discontinuous).

## Nepal-specific suspicions — answered

1. **Teacher-guide companion question:** the Grade 10 maths guide IS a
   true companion to the held Grade 10 student book (same 18 units, same
   order, same titles, cites the student book's exercises by number, one
   shared author). It is NOT a mismatch like the English guides. Caveats:
   it is a 2074 DRAFT, and 7 of its pages are blank. No Grade 9 teacher
   guide exists in this set at all.
2. **"2079" for Grade 9:** PRINTED in the Grade 9 MATHS book (imprint
   p. 2, "प्रथम संस्करण : वि.सं २०७९"). The record is right for maths;
   the English Grade 9 "2079" problem does not repeat here.
3. **Grade 10's 218 pages vs Grade 9's 384:** the Grade 10 book is
   COMPLETE — printed pp. 1–215 continuous, all 18 units plus a full
   answer key (pp. 207–215). The short length is real: Grade 10 is a
   consolidation year with shorter units and no long applied units.
4. **Optional books:** separate works, not continuations — English
   medium, First Editions 2082 BS (Grade 9) and 2083 BS (Grade 10),
   written to the new Secondary Level Optional Mathematics Curriculum
   (Grade 9–10), 2081. They are the only place in the band where
   functions, matrices, vectors, trigonometry beyond right triangles,
   limits and continuity are taught.

## Notes for the country file (not findings, just pointers)

- Time-sensitive content (do not teach as current fact without checking
  the date): G9 Unit 4 tariff tables (electricity/water/telephone/taxi,
  2079-era); G10 Unit 2 exchange-rate table (Nepal Rastra Bank rates for
  2072 Pus 20); G9 Unit 2 income-tax treatment and the 13% VAT rate.
- Nepali land units (ropani/aana/paisa/daam, katha/dhur) are a real
  teaching strand in G9 Unit 5, with conversions (1 ropani = 16 aana,
  1 aana = 31.79 sq. m).
- The compulsory books' statistics/probability/trigonometry ceilings
  (G9: mean/median/mode/quartiles, empirical probability, special-angle
  trig; G10: grouped mean/median/quartiles, addition/multiplication
  laws, height/distance) are the correct "what Nepal teaches" for the
  compulsory course; the Optional books raise the ceiling to functions,
  matrices, vectors, identities, limits and continuity.
- No book in this band teaches logarithms, complex numbers, differential
  equations, differentiation/integration, or probability distributions.
  If the 52-lesson maths spine needs those topics for Grades 9–10, Nepal
  contributes nothing to them from this band.

A = [[2,3],[1,4]], |A| = 5, A⁻¹ = (1/5)[[4,−3],[−1,2]] (correct);
A = [[4,3],[1,2]] is the matrix of Activity 1, where the properties
are to be verified, not worked out — its inverse is not computed in
the book. Guide companion evidence (3) corrected: the guide's worked
examples do NOT reuse the student book's numbers or contexts (its Unit
1 survey is 300 students, not the student book's 52-student
cricket/football survey). G09-Optional "No translation" softened to

**Checker notes carried forward:** the G09 unit-table error is
systematic (11 of 20 rows), not a typo — the reader decoded the TOC's
Preeti digits from the text layer and got systematically wrong ranges,
even though the same ranges were then contradicted by the reader's own
correct exercise-page findings. "Stamp Duty" appears nowhere in the G10
student book or the guide; both call the unit "Tax and Money Exchange".
The G10 guide's DRAFT watermark is on every page. The G10-Optional
filing writes "Comparison" where the book prints "Comparision"
(§11.2.2 heading), and Fisher as "Father of Statistics" where the book
says "Father of Modern Statistics" — spelling/wording as filed, not
book errors.

## Unverified by check — carried, never cited as fact

**NOTHING is unverified — 0 not-found anchors.** All 452 anchors were
verified on their cited pages. The following limits on what the filing
rests on are stated plainly so a course builder knows exactly what is
behind each claim:

- **Two partial held copies.** The G3 English 2074 file's Lessons 1–4
  (printed pp. 1–16) and its Lesson 29 tail (mixed exercise) are absent
  from that file — but their content is present in the Nepali book and
  the older English edition of the same book, both read in full, so no
  Grade 3 content is unverified. No check anchor fell in the missing
  Lessons 1–4 or beyond the truncation point; the absence itself was
  verified as filed. The G5 student book's Unit 1 Geometry (printed
  pp. 1–17) is absent from the held copy and is NOT covered by any other
  held file — the G5 guide is keyed to a different textbook and must not
  be used as a substitute. What the full Grade 5 book teaches in Unit 1
  is therefore unverified from this set; everything else in the G5
  student book (printed pp. 18–136) was read in full.
- **Method limits (visual/OCR reads where stated).** The Devanagari
  books were read from renders + tesseract `nep` OCR or from
  Preeti-converted text layers, with key pages verified visually — see
  "How this filing was made". Picture-heavy worksheet pages in the G2
  Nepali book (e.g. printed pp. 54–59, 61–62) OCR thinly and are
  described from what the OCR and page structure show, not guessed; the
  G2 Nepali p. 90 balancing-weights exercise could not be pinned down
  from OCR alone and no specific weights are asserted for it. The G5
  and G8 teacher guides were read at lesson-intro + plan-table +
  sampled-activity depth, not page by page. The G10 teacher guide's 7
  blank pages (printed pp. 17, 19, 181–185) hold no content in the held
  copy.
- **No anchor was left NOT FOUND in any band.** Nothing in this filing
  is carried as an unverified claim.
